# Management-Governance Module — AKAM (Synthesis)

## Abstract

Akamai’s leaders look competent but weakly aligned. Capital deployment has not proven per-share value creation: FY2021–FY2025 cash acquisitions were $598.8m+$872.1m+$106.2m+$434.1m+$55.1m=$2.066bn, and H1 buybacks of $615.744m were 278% of $221.174m cash after total capex. [Capital IQ Financials, Cash Flow, FY2021–FY2025 — Tier-5 vendor; Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, p.7] Pay disclosure is incomplete; 94.4% of available grants were service-based, founder-CEO ownership was 1.9%, and observed sales were plan-driven. [FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 18, pp.84–86; Web: 2026 DEF 14A, pp.26–27, filed 2026-03-31 — unverified] The largest risk is that repeated acquisitions, including another $205m for LayerX, lack disclosed deal-level returns. [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 6, p.17] The verdict is misaligned or weak stewardship, with no hard disqualifier but material evidence gaps.

## 1. Stewardship Verdict

- **Verdict:** Misaligned or weak stewardship
- **NON-NEGOTIABLE GATE: PASS** — 116 of 179 checklist items were answered. No hard disqualifier, Critical red flag, Disqualifying-graded controller/CEO/CFO/chair, or material undisclosed legal matter was found in the completed, coverage-limited work.
- **Hard disqualifier flagged (business-model/01)?** N — “No disqualifier triggered.” [Business-Model Disqualifier Scan, AKAM, Verdict-Lock Signal, 2026-09-14]
- Reporting regime: U.S. issuer listed on Nasdaq, U.S. GAAP, fiscal year ending December 31. [FY2025 Form 10-K, cover and Item 8, filed 2026-02-20]
- Management quality /100: **56**
- Capital allocation /100: **38**
- Incentive alignment /100: **43**
- Shareholder friendliness /100: **60**
- Disclosure candor /100: **54**
- People & network integrity /100: **59**
- Audit & assurance quality /100: **51**
- RPT (related-party transaction) & leakage risk /100 **(higher = worse): 45**
- Contingent-liability risk /100 **(higher = worse): 37**
- Accounting-forensics risk /100 **(higher = worse): 38**
- Legal & regulatory risk /100 **(higher = worse): 40** — the specialist’s 32 was raised to the mandatory 40 floor because the legal-database sweep was coverage-limited.
- Governance risk /100 **(higher = worse): 60** — the pre-cap synthesis read was 50 because the six fired flags are High rather than Critical and no controller/integrity lock was found; the serial-acquirer rule raises it to its 60 floor.
- Data quality /100: **58** — 16 source files and 38 workbook tabs had no extraction failure, but the frozen evidence omits the proxy, compensation, complete ownership/insider, RPT, voting, and auditor-fee records. [Governance Data Triage, AKAM, §§1 and 5–6, 2026-09-14]
- Overall usefulness /100: **68** — all expanded specialists ran and the 179-item registry is complete, but the no-proxy cap limits usefulness to 70.
- **Checklist coverage:** **116/179 (64.8%)** — 40 Green / 67 Amber / 9 Red / 63 not available or insufficient.
- Insider ownership (one line): Founder-CEO F. Thomson Leighton held 1.9%, and all directors/officers held 2.3%, as of 2026-02-20; 19,600 observed open-market shares were sold under Rule 10b5-1 plans, no observed open-market purchase was found, and the complete record and actual pledge balance remain unavailable. [Web: 2026 DEF 14A, Security Ownership, pp.26–27, filed 2026-03-31 — unverified public SEC filing supplement; Web: SEC Forms 4, 2025-11-07 to 2026-07-15 — unverified public filing sweep]
- Biggest governance signal (one line): FY2021–FY2025 cash acquisitions were $598.8m + $872.1m + $106.2m + $434.1m + $55.1m = $2.066bn, another $205m went to LayerX without a disclosed deal-level return record, and H1 buybacks of $615.744m were 278.4% of $221.174m cash from operations after total capex while shares outstanding fell by only 1.120m, or 0.8%. [Capital IQ Financials, Cash Flow sheet, FY2021–FY2025 — Tier-5 vendor; Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 6, p.17, Statements of Cash Flows, p.7, and Statements of Stockholders’ Equity, pp.9–12]
- **Checklist Risk = max(45, 37, 38, 40) = 45**, set by RPT & leakage risk; the missing RPT ledger prevents a low-risk conclusion.
- **Governance Score /100 = 50** (unrounded 50.29) = 0.14×38 + 0.11×43 + 0.11×60 + 0.10×54 + 0.11×56 + 0.09×51 + 0.11×59 + 0.11×(100−60) + 0.12×(100−45).
- **Confidence-Adjusted Governance Score /100 = 33** (unrounded 32.69) = 50.29 × 65 / 100.
- **Governance Rating:** Weak
- **Confidence Score /100:** 65 — audited/current filings support the financial and audit facts, while the public-proxy supplement and incomplete legal, registry, sanctions, and ownership coverage prevent a higher score.
- **Red-Flag Count / Critical Red-Flag Count:** **6 / 0**

Free cash flow (FCF) means cash from operations (CFO) minus total capex. “Strict” net debt means total debt minus cash equivalents; net debt/EBITDA divides that figure by earnings before interest, tax, depreciation, and amortization. Earnings per share (EPS) is profit allocated per diluted share; GAAP is the reported accounting basis, while non-GAAP removes company-selected items. Stock-based compensation (SBC) is employee pay settled in shares or share-linked awards; profit after tax (PAT) is bottom-line profit, and key management personnel (KMP) are the senior people who direct the company.

## 1A. Module Disconfirmation

- **Strongest bear point:** FY2021–FY2025 cash acquisitions were $598.8m + $872.1m + $106.2m + $434.1m + $55.1m = $2.066bn, LayerX added $205m, and the admitted evidence contains no deal-by-deal return on invested capital (ROIC—the profit earned per dollar invested), synergy-delivery, impairment, or alternative-use comparison; H1 buybacks of $615.744m were 278.4% of $221.174m strict FCF while shares outstanding fell by 1.120m, or 0.8%. [Capital IQ Financials, Cash Flow sheet, FY2021–FY2025 — Tier-5 vendor; Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 6, p.17, Statements of Cash Flows, p.7, and Statements of Stockholders’ Equity, pp.9–12]
- **Strongest bull point:** Q2 2026 revenue, non-GAAP EPS, and non-GAAP margin all landed inside management’s prior ranges; H1 repurchases reduced shares outstanding by 1.120 million, and the FY2025 audit and internal-control-over-financial-reporting (ICFR) opinions were unqualified. [Q1 FY2026 earnings release, Financial Guidance, p.2; Q2 FY2026 earnings release, Results, p.1; Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Statements of Stockholders’ Equity, pp.9–12; FY2025 Form 10-K, pp.51–52, 94]
- **Single killer risk:** Capital continues to move into acquisitions, cloud infrastructure, and buybacks without returns above the cost of capital, leaving minority holders with higher leverage and no proven per-share value gain.
- **Disconfirming evidence already visible:** H1 share count fell 0.8%, the new convertible notes have zero coupons, and CIQ reported 32.6× LTM EBITDA/interest coverage; these facts weaken an immediate financial-distress interpretation but do not establish sound capital returns. [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Statements of Stockholders’ Equity, pp.9–12 and Note 7, pp.17–20; ciq_facts.json, interest_coverage_x, 2026-09-14 — Tier-5 vendor]

| governance-data-triage | Partial; current primary evidence is useful, but core governance records are absent from the frozen pool. | The FY2026 DEF 14A is the single highest-value missing document because it carries pay, ownership, board, RPT, auditor-fee, voting, and rights evidence. |
| management-and-track-record | Mixed; stable founder-led team, but only one completed guidance interval and no proven 2027 delivery record. | Q2 revenue/EPS/margin met the prior guide, while FY2026 revenue and EPS high ends were reduced and the margin range widened down. |
| capital-allocation-scorecard | Weak; per-share value creation is not proven. | FY2021–FY2025 cash acquisitions of $598.8m + $872.1m + $106.2m + $434.1m + $55.1m = $2.066bn, plus $205m for LayerX, lack disclosed deal returns, triggering RF-CAP-004. |
| incentives-and-compensation | Partial and weakly aligned; 43/100 under the proxy cap. | FY2025 grants were 94.42% service-based, annual burn was 4.73%, overhang was 13.04%, and executive RSUs can conditionally vest on a single trigger. |
| ownership-and-insider-behavior | Insufficient for a full alignment score. | CEO ownership was 1.9%, the director/officer group 2.3%, and the observed 19,600 sold shares were all plan-driven; complete activity and pledge data are missing. |
| board-and-shareholder-rights | Board independent; rights are mixed and constrained. | Eight of nine directors were classified independent, but 41.9% of votes opposed the 8.0 million-share stock-plan increase. |
| candor-and-disclosure-quality | Mixed; adverse facts are disclosed, but adjusted framing changes the economic picture. | Q2 non-GAAP operating income of $270.7 million exceeded GAAP operating income of $80.3 million by $190.4 million, or 237% of GAAP. |
| people-integrity-dossiers | Coverage-limited; no disqualifying or material personal-integrity fact was identified. | Twenty-four named people were graded Clean on an own-record, coverage-limited basis; one unnamed CSO and registry/legal gaps remain. |
| audit-and-assurance-quality | Mixed; current opinions are clean, but independence and component coverage are incomplete. | PwC’s FY2025 opinions were unqualified, while its unrelated 2024 PCAOB independence-quality-control censure triggers RF-AUD-007. |
| related-party-and-group-forensics | Mixed/unknown leakage risk. | Zero FY2025 Item 404(a) transactions were reported, but aggregate RPT dollars are unavailable and 46 subsidiaries lack a cash-by-entity map. |
| contingent-liabilities-and-commitments | Moderate, concentrated in lease commitments. | $1.8659bn scheduled lease payments + $278.0m uncommenced colocation leases + $187.1m final data-center commitment = $2.331bn, or 46.8% of FY2025 net worth; $1.570bn is already recognized and $731.2m of future sublease income is separate. |
| accounting-forensics | Mixed risk; no Beneish/Dechow battery-red condition. | Beneish M was −2.750, but recurring restructuring is Red and strict net debt/EBITDA reached 5.14× at Q2 2026. |
| regulatory-legal-and-compliance | Coverage-limited watch; no current hard-lock matter confirmed. | Historic 2016 FCPA-related conduct is resolved; the current concern is a reported two-notch S&P decline in 54 days, below the three-notch trigger. |

## 3. Reconciliation

- **Dedicated capital-allocation work supersedes the quick read.** The business-model quick read scored capital allocation at 45 and did not apply the serial-acquirer cap. The dedicated specialist examined the five-year cash-acquisition record, LayerX, buyback funding, filed convertible principal, return-on-capital trend, and missing deal returns; its 38 score and RF-CAP-004 therefore govern this synthesis. [Business-Model Capital Allocation Governance, AKAM, 2026-09-14; Capital Allocation Scorecard, AKAM, §§1–5, 2026-09-14]
- **Ownership alignment is not the same as shareholder-rights structure.** Agent 04 refused to assign a numerical ownership-alignment score because the complete transaction and pledge data are absent. Agent 05 could assess a public-proxy board and rights structure, but its raw 67 was capped at 60. This synthesis uses 60 for shareholder friendliness while preserving ownership alignment as insufficient.
- **The subsidiary count is 46, not 57.** Agent 07 initially counted 57 entries. Agent 09 re-counted the primary Exhibit 21.1 and found 46 legal entities; the primary-source count wins, with no omission inferred. [Related-Party & Group Forensics, AKAM, finding 09-019, 2026-09-14]
- **Noname identity is resolved, not cleared.** Agent 07 left Noname Gate Ltd. and “Noname Security” pending. Agent 12 matched the latter to the acquired business/trade label of Noname Gate, not a predecessor of Akamai; Israeli registry, primary court, and sanctions coverage remain incomplete, so neither row becomes resolved-clean. [Regulatory, Legal & Compliance, AKAM, §1A, 2026-09-14]
- **The legal sweep adds a sub-material case without changing the contingency conclusion.** Agent 10 had filings-only litigation coverage. Agent 12 found a secondary-source report of the $3.741 million Barkan claim, 0.079% of Q2 equity, but no primary docket; it stays Amber and does not trigger RF-CMP-001. [Regulatory, Legal & Compliance, AKAM, §§1A and 8, 2026-09-14]
- **Candor’s filing-timeliness input remains unproven.** Agent 06 used a neutral placeholder because agent 12 had not yet completed its work; agent 12 later verified only one of the required 12 quarters. The 54 candor score is retained, but no positive timeliness claim is carried.

## 5. Stewardship Summary

Management has a stable operating bench and met the one completed quarterly guide, but a single interval cannot prove a durable promises-versus-delivery record. [Management & Track Record, AKAM, §§2–5, 2026-09-14] Capital use has not yet shown per-share value creation: acquisitions lack disclosed return scorecards, buybacks exceeded strict free cash flow, GAAP diluted EPS fell from $3.52 in FY2023 to $3.07 in FY2025, and return on capital declined on the vendor series. [Capital Allocation Scorecard, AKAM, §§1–5, 2026-09-14] The available incentive evidence points more toward retention and size than per-share returns, while 1.9% founder-CEO ownership gives exposure without control and the complete ownership record is missing. [Incentives & Compensation, AKAM, §§2–4; Ownership & Insider Behavior, AKAM, §§1–5, 2026-09-14] Minority holders have an independent chair and one-share/one-vote structure, but the 41.9% stock-plan opposition, conditional single-trigger vesting, takeover limits, and adjusted-earnings emphasis weaken protection. [Board & Shareholder Rights, AKAM, §§1–5; Candor & Disclosure Quality, AKAM, §§2–5, 2026-09-14] The main reason not to entrust more capital without proof is the combination of repeated acquisitions, rising leverage, and no deal-level return record. [Capital Allocation Scorecard, AKAM, §5, 2026-09-14]

## 6. What Would Change The Stewardship Verdict?

| Current Verdict | What Would Strengthen It | What Would Weaken It | Data Needed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Misaligned or weak stewardship | Two to three years of deal-level returns above the cost of capital; strict FCF covering buybacks; GAAP EPS and return on capital improving; lower equity burn; a board response to the 41.9% dissent; full legal/person checks without adverse findings | Another debt-funded acquisition without a return hurdle; impairments or divestitures exposing overpayment; strict leverage staying above 5×; buybacks continuing above FCF; widening GAAP-adjusted gaps; a material undisclosed matter or adverse person finding | First: frozen FY2026 DEF 14A. Then deal scorecards, complete Form 4/13D/13G and pledge data, current Form AP and fee table, intercompany/RPT ledger, official legal/registry/sanctions results, and the next annual lease schedule |

## 7. Note To The Final Synthesizer

- Treat the stewardship verdict as **misaligned or weak**: FY2021–FY2025 cash acquisitions were $598.8m + $872.1m + $106.2m + $434.1m + $55.1m = $2.066bn, and LayerX added $205m, without disclosed deal-level returns.
- **Non-Negotiable Gate: PASS, with 116/179 checklist items answered.** No hard disqualifier, Critical flag, Disqualifying controller/KMP, or material undisclosed legal matter was confirmed.
- Capital deployment has not proven per-share value creation: H1 share count fell, but buybacks were 278.4% of strict FCF, GAAP diluted EPS fell from $3.52 to $3.07 in FY2023–FY2025, and vendor return on capital fell.
- Incentives and ownership do not prove alignment with minority holders: 94.42% of FY2025 grant units were service-based, burn was 4.73%, conditional single-trigger vesting exists, and founder-CEO ownership was 1.9%.
- **People read:** 24 Clean, coverage-limited; 0 Minor, 0 Material, 0 Disqualifying; one unnamed CSO. Aaron Ahola is the clearest named monitor because his Nasdaq council recusal controls are unverified, not because an adverse record was found. No open §24 Filter 1 integrity signal, and no person trips a gate. [People Integrity Dossiers, AKAM, §§1–2, 2026-09-14]
- **Checklist read:** 64.8% answered; nine Red items — A4-02 audit-firm calibre, A4-13 firm/partner discipline, A6-04 equity burn/overhang, A6-05 conditional single-trigger vesting, A6-09 problematic pay practice, A7a-01 lease-related commitments, A8-05 recurring restructuring, A10-03 stock-plan dissent, and A14-01 leverage. RPT & leakage risk at 45 is the binding Checklist Risk because the aggregate RPT ledger and group cash topology are missing.
- The biggest governance flag is RF-CAP-004, High: repeated acquisitions without return proof; it caps capital allocation at 50 and floors Governance risk at 60.
- Business-model/01 hard-disqualifier result, verbatim: **“No disqualifier triggered.”**
- §24 rejector filters: serial acquirer tripped, applying CapAlloc max 50 and GovRisk floor 60; turnaround, structurally unaligned owner, and unresolved-integrity filters did not trip.
- Partial-data caps apply to incentive alignment, usefulness, shareholder friendliness, board reliance, audit assurance, RPT risk, people/network integrity, legal risk, and confidence. The legal floor moved LegalRisk from 32 to 40; other capped scores were already inside their limits.
- The single highest-value next data request is the filed FY2026 DEF 14A in the frozen evidence set.
- **This module supersedes business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance. Treat this module’s governance verdict and scores as the primary governance read.**
- Preserve RF-DISC-002 in the cross-module forensic roll-up; recurring SBC/restructuring and the large GAAP-to-adjusted gap may compound with earnings-quality or balance-sheet flags.

## 8. Simple Summary

- Management met the one completed quarterly guide, but one quarter is not a long record. [Q1 FY2026 earnings release, Financial Guidance, p.2; Q2 FY2026 earnings release, Results, p.1]
- Capital has not proven per-share value creation; acquisitions lack return scorecards and buybacks exceeded strict FCF. [Capital IQ Financials, Cash Flow sheet, FY2021–FY2025 — Tier-5 vendor; Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, p.7]
- The available pay evidence mainly rewards service and retention, with high burn and conditional single-trigger vesting. [FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 18, pp.84–86 and Exhibit 10.5, §6(c)–(e)]
- The founder-CEO owns 1.9%; observed sales were plan-driven, no observed open-market buy was found, and the full record is missing. [Web: 2026 DEF 14A, pp.26–27, filed 2026-03-31 — unverified public SEC filing supplement; Web: SEC Forms 4, 2025-11-07 to 2026-07-15 — unverified public filing sweep]
- An independent chair, 8/9 independent directors, and one-share/one-vote help minority holders, but stock-plan dissent and takeover limits weaken the read. [Web: 2026 DEF 14A, pp.27–29 and Appendix C-1, filed 2026-03-31 — unverified public SEC filing supplement; Web: SEC Form 8-K, Item 5.07, 2026-05-13 — unverified public filing supplement]
- Management discloses the GAAP bridge, but recurring exclusions make adjusted performance look much better. [Q2 FY2026 Supplemental Financial Information, GAAP-to-Non-GAAP Reconciliation sheet]
- No hard disqualifier or Critical red flag was confirmed; six High flags remain. [Business-Model Disqualifier Scan, Verdict-Lock Signal; Management-Governance Red-Flag Register, 2026-09-14]
- The module is useful but capped: current filings support the core negative capital-allocation call, while the missing proxy and incomplete legal/network work limit confidence.
